Mūlapariyāyasuttaṃ

Majjhima Nikāya 1
← Back to Majjhima Nikāya
1
1. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharati subhagavane sālarājamūle. Tatra kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘‘bhikkhavo’’ti. ‘‘Bhadante’’ti te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca – ‘‘sabbadhammamūlapariyāyaṃ vo, bhikkhave, desessāmi. Taṃ suṇātha, sādhukaṃ manasi karotha, bhāsissāmī’’ti. ‘‘Evaṃ, bhante’’ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –
Thus have I heard. At one time the Blessed One (bhagavān) was dwelling in Ukkaṭṭhā in the Subhaga Grove at the foot of a royal sal tree (sālarājamūle).

There the Blessed One (bhagavān) addressed the monks (bhikkhus), saying: “Monks (bhikkhavo)!”

“Venerable sir (bhadante),” those monks (bhikkhus) replied to the Blessed One (bhagavato).

The Blessed One (bhagavā) said this: “Monks (bhikkhave), I will teach you the Discourse on the Root of All Things (sabbadhammamūlapariyāyaṃ).

Listen to that, pay close attention (sādhukaṃ manasi karotha), I will speak.”

“As you say, venerable sir (evaṃ bhante),” those monks (bhikkhus) replied to the Blessed One (bhagavato).

The Blessed One (bhagavā) said this:
1. Yaṃ panetaṃ ‘‘evaṃ me suta’’ntiādikaṃ nidānaṃ. Tattha evanti nipātapadaṃ. Metiādīni nāmapadāni. Ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharatīti ettha vīti upasaggapadaṃ, haratīti ākhyātapadanti iminā tāva nayena padavibhāgo veditabbo. Atthato pana evaṃ-saddo tāva upamūpadesasampahaṃsanagarahaṇavacanasampaṭiggahākāranidassanāvadhāraṇādianekatthappabhedo. Tathāhesa – ‘‘evaṃ jātena maccena kattabbaṃ kusalaṃ bahu’’nti evamādīsu (dha. pa. 53) upamāyaṃ āgato. ‘‘Evaṃ te abhikkamitabbaṃ, evaṃ te paṭikkamitabba’’ntiādīsu (a. ni. 4.122) upadese. ‘‘Evametaṃ bhagavā, evametaṃ sugatā’’tiādīsu (a. ni. 3.66) sampahaṃsane. ‘‘Evamevaṃ panāyaṃ vasalī yasmiṃ vā tasmiṃ vā tassa muṇḍakassa samaṇakassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsatī’’tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 1.187) garahaṇe. ‘‘Evaṃ bhanteti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosu’’ntiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.1) vacanasampaṭiggahe. ‘‘Evaṃ byākho ahaṃ, bhante, bhagavatā dhammaṃ desitaṃ ājānāmī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.398) ākāre. ‘‘Ehi tvaṃ, māṇavaka, yena samaṇo ānando tenupasaṅkama, upasaṅkamitvā mama vacanena samaṇaṃ, ānandaṃ, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ puccha – ‘subho māṇavo todeyyaputto, bhavantaṃ ānandaṃ, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ pucchatī’ti, evañca vadehi sādhu kira bhavaṃ ānando yena subhassa māṇavassa todeyyaputtassa nivesanaṃ, tenupasaṅkamatu anukampaṃ upādāyā’’tiādīsu (dī. ni. 1.445) nidassane. ‘‘Taṃ kiṃ maññatha, kālāmā, ime dhammā kusalā vā akusalā vāti? Akusalā, bhante. Sāvajjā vā anavajjā vāti? Sāvajjā, bhante. Viññugarahitā vā viññuppasatthā vāti? Viññugarahitā, bhante. Samattā samādinnā ahitāya dukkhāya saṃvattanti no vā, kathaṃ vo ettha hotīti? Samattā, bhante, samādinnā ahitāya dukkhāya saṃvattanti, evaṃ no ettha hotī’’tiādīsu (a. ni. 3.66) avadhāraṇe. Svāyamidha ākāranidassanāvadhāraṇesu daṭṭhabbo. evaṃ Tattha ākāraṭṭhena evaṃsaddena etamatthaṃ dīpeti – nānānayanipuṇaṃ anekajjhāsayasamuṭṭhānaṃ atthabyañjanasampannaṃ vividhapāṭihāriyaṃ dhammatthadesanāpaṭivedhagambhīraṃ sabbasattānaṃ sakasakabhāsānurūpato sotapathamāgacchantaṃ tassa bhagavato vacanaṃ sabbappakārena ko samattho viññātuṃ, sabbathāmena pana sotukāmataṃ janetvāpi evaṃ me sutaṃ, mayāpi ekenākārena sutanti. evaṃ Nidassanaṭṭhena ‘‘nāhaṃ sayambhū, na mayā idaṃ sacchikata’’nti attānaṃ parimocento evaṃ me sutaṃ, mayāpi evaṃ sutanti idāni vattabbaṃ sakalaṃ suttaṃ nidasseti. Avadhāraṇaṭṭhena ‘‘etadaggaṃ, bhikkhave, mama sāvakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ bahussutānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando, gatimantānaṃ, satimantānaṃ, dhitimantānaṃ, upaṭṭhākānaṃ yadidaṃ ānando’’ti (a. ni. 1.219-223) evaṃ bhagavatā, ‘‘āyasmā ānando atthakusalo dhammakusalo byañjanakusalo niruttikusalo pubbāparakusalo’’ti (a. ni. 5.169) evaṃ dhammasenāpatinā ca pasatthabhāvānurūpaṃ attano dhāraṇabalaṃ dassento sattānaṃ sotukamyataṃ janeti ‘‘evaṃ me sutaṃ, tañca kho atthato vā byañjanato vā anūnamanadhikaṃ, evameva na aññathā daṭṭhabba’’nti. Me-saddo tīsu atthesu dissati. Tathā hissa ‘‘gāthābhigītaṃ me abhojaneyya’’ntiādīsu (su. ni. 81) mayāti attho. ‘‘Sādhu me, bhante bhagavā, saṃkhittena dhammaṃ desetū’’tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 4.88) mayhanti attho. ‘‘Dhammadāyādā me, bhikkhave, bhavathā’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.29) mamāti attho. Idha pana ‘‘mayā suta’’nti ca ‘‘mama suta’’nti ca atthadvaye yujjati. Sutanti ayaṃ suta-saddo saupasaggo ca anupasaggo ca gamana-vissuta-kilinna-upacitānuyoga-sotaviññeyya-sotadvārānusāraviññātādianekatthappabhedo. Tathā hissa ‘‘senāya pasuto’’tiādīsu gacchantoti attho. ‘‘Sutadhammassa passato’’tiādīsu (udā. 11) vissutadhammassāti attho, ‘‘avassutā avassutassāti’’ādīsu (pāci. 657) kilinnā kilinnassāti attho. ‘‘Tumhehi puññaṃ pasutaṃ anappaka’’ntiādīsu (khu. pā. 7.12) upacitanti attho. ‘‘Ye jhānapasutā dhīrā’’tiādīsu (dha. pa. 181) jhānānuyuttāti attho. ‘‘Diṭṭhaṃ sutaṃ muta’’ntiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.241) sotaviññeyyanti attho. ‘‘Sutadharo sutasannicayo’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.339) sotadvārānusāraviññātadharoti attho. Idha panassa sotadvārānusārena ‘‘upadhārita’’nti vā ‘‘upadhāraṇa’’nti vāti attho. Me-saddassa hi mayāti atthe sati ‘‘evaṃ mayā sutaṃ sotadvārānusārena upadhārita’’nti yujjati. Mamāti atthe sati ‘‘evaṃ mama sutaṃ sotadvārānusārena upadhāraṇa’’nti yujjati. Suta Evametesu tīsu padesu evanti sotaviññāṇādiviññāṇakiccanidassanaṃ. Meti vuttaviññāṇasamaṅgipuggalanidassanaṃ. Sutanti assavanabhāvappaṭikkhepato anūnānadhikāviparītaggahaṇanidassanaṃ. Tathā evanti tassā sotadvārānusārena pavattāya viññāṇavīthiyā nānappakārena ārammaṇe pavattibhāvappakāsanaṃ. Meti attappakāsanaṃ. Sutanti dhammappakāsanaṃ. Ayañhettha saṅkhepo ‘‘nānappakārena ārammaṇe pavattāya viññāṇavīthiyā mayā na aññaṃ kataṃ, idaṃ pana kataṃ, ayaṃ dhammo suto’’ti. Suta Suta Tathā evanti niddisitabbappakāsanaṃ. Meti puggalappakāsanaṃ. Sutanti puggalakiccappakāsanaṃ. Idaṃ vuttaṃ hoti – yaṃ suttaṃ niddisissāmi, taṃ mayā evaṃ sutanti. Suta Tathā evanti yassa cittasantānassa nānākārappavattiyā nānatthabyañjanaggahaṇaṃ hoti, tassa nānākāraniddeso. Evanti hi ayaṃ ākārapaññatti, meti kattuniddeso, sutanti visayaniddeso. Ettāvatā nānākārappavattena cittasantānena taṃsamaṅgino kattuvisaye gahaṇasanniṭṭhānaṃ kataṃ hoti. Eva me suta Atha vā evanti puggalakiccaniddeso. Sutanti viññāṇakiccaniddeso. Meti ubhayakiccayuttapuggalaniddeso. Ayaṃ panettha saṅkhepo – mayā savanakiccaviññāṇasamaṅginā puggalena viññāṇavasena laddhasavanakiccavohārena sutanti. Suta Tattha evanti ca meti ca saccikaṭṭhaparamatthavasena avijjamānapaññatti. Kiñhettha taṃ paramatthato atthi, yaṃ evanti vā meti vā niddesaṃ labhetha? Sutanti vijjamānapaññatti. Yañhi tamettha sotena upaladdhaṃ, taṃ paramatthato vijjamānanti. me Suta Tathā evanti ca meti ca taṃ taṃ upādāya vattabbato upādāpaññatti. Sutanti diṭṭhādīni upanidhāya vattabbato upanidhāpaññatti. Ettha ca evanti vacanena asammohaṃ dīpeti. Na hi sammūḷho nānappakārapaṭivedhasamattho hoti. Sutanti vacanena sutassa asammosaṃ dīpeti. Yassa hi sutaṃ sammuṭṭhaṃ hoti , na so kālantarena mayā sutanti paṭijānāti. Iccassa asammohena paññāsiddhi, asammosena pana satisiddhi. Tattha paññā pubbaṅgamāya satiyā byañjanāvadhāraṇasamatthatā, satipubbaṅgamāya paññāya atthapaṭivedhasamatthatā, tadubhayasamatthatāyogena atthabyañjanasampannassa dhammakosassa anupālanasamatthato dhammabhaṇḍāgārikattasiddhi. me Suta Suta Aparo nayo – evanti vacanena yoniso manasikāraṃ dīpeti, ayoniso manasikaroto hi nānappakārapaṭivedhābhāvato. Sutanti vacanena avikkhepaṃ dīpeti, vikkhittacittassa savanābhāvato. Tathā hi vikkhittacitto puggalo sabbasampattiyā vuccamānopi ‘‘na mayā sutaṃ, puna bhaṇathā’’ti bhaṇati. Yoniso manasikārena cettha attasammāpaṇidhiṃ pubbe ca katapuññataṃ sādheti, sammā apaṇihitattassa pubbe akatapuññassa vā tadabhāvato. Avikkhepena pana saddhammassavanaṃ sappurisūpanissayañca sādheti. Na hi vikkhittacitto sotuṃ sakkoti, na ca sappurise anupassayamānassa savanaṃ atthīti. Suta Aparo nayo – yasmā evanti yassa cittasantānassa nānākārappavattiyā nānatthabyañjanaggahaṇaṃ hoti, tassa nānākāraniddesoti vuttaṃ. So ca evaṃ bhaddako ākāro na sammā appaṇihitattano pubbe akatapuññassa vā hoti, tasmā evanti iminā bhaddakena ākārena pacchimacakkadvayasampattiṃ attano dīpeti, sutanti savanayogena purimacakkadvayasampattiṃ. Na hi appatirūpadese vasato sappurisūpanissayavirahitassa vā savanaṃ atthi. Iccassa pacchimacakkadvayasiddhiyā āsayasuddhi siddhā hoti. Purimacakkadvayasiddhiyā payogasuddhi. Tāya ca āsayasuddhiyā adhigamabyattisiddhi, payogasuddhiyā āgamabyattisiddhi. Iti payogāsayasuddhassa āgamādhigamasampannassa vacanaṃ aruṇuggaṃ viya sūriyassa udayato yonisomanasikāro viya ca kusalakammassa arahati bhagavato vacanassa pubbaṅgamaṃ bhavitunti ṭhāne nidānaṃ ṭhapento evaṃ me sutantiādimāha. suta evaṃ me suta Aparo nayo – evanti iminā nānappakārapaṭivedhadīpakena vacanena attano atthapaṭibhānapaṭisambhidāsampattisabbhāvaṃ dīpeti. Sutanti iminā sotabbabhedapaṭivedhadīpakena dhammaniruttipaṭisambhidāsampattisabbhāvaṃ . Evanti ca idaṃ yonisomanasikāradīpakaṃ vacanaṃ bhāsamāno – ‘‘ete mayā dhammā manasānupekkhitā diṭṭhiyā suppaṭividdhā’’ti dīpeti. Sutanti idaṃ savanayogadīpakaṃ vacanaṃ bhāsamāno – ‘‘bahū mayā dhammā sutā dhātā vacasā paricitā’’ti dīpeti. Tadubhayenapi atthabyañjanapāripūriṃ dīpento savane ādaraṃ janeti. Atthabyañjanaparipuṇṇañhi dhammaṃ ādarena assuṇanto mahatā hitā paribāhiro hotīti ādaraṃ janetvā sakkaccaṃ dhammo sotabboti. Suta Eva Suta ‘‘Evaṃ me suta’’nti iminā pana sakalena vacanena āyasmā ānando tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammaṃ attano adahanto asappurisabhūmiṃ atikkamati, sāvakattaṃ paṭijānanto sappurisabhūmiṃ okkamati. Tathā asaddhammā cittaṃ vuṭṭhāpeti, saddhamme cittaṃ patiṭṭhāpeti. ‘‘Kevalaṃ sutamevetaṃ mayā tasseva pana bhagavato vacana’’nti dīpento attānaṃ parimoceti, satthāraṃ apadisati, jinavacanaṃ appeti, dhammanettiṃ patiṭṭhāpeti. Apica ‘‘evaṃ me suta’’nti attanā uppāditabhāvaṃ appaṭijānanto purimavacanaṃ vivaranto ‘‘sammukhā paṭiggahitamidaṃ mayā tassa bhagavato catuvesārajjavisāradassa dasabaladharassa āsabhaṭṭhānaṭṭhāyino sīhanādanādino sabbasattuttamassa dhammissarassa dhammarājassa dhammādhipatino dhammadīpassa dhammasaraṇassa saddhammavaracakkavattino sammāsambuddhassa vacanaṃ, na ettha atthe vā dhamme vā pade vā byañjane vā kaṅkhā vā vimati vā kattabbā’’ti sabbadevamanussānaṃ imasmiṃ dhamme assaddhiyaṃ vināseti, saddhāsampadaṃ uppādetīti. Tenetaṃ vuccati – ‘‘Vināsayati assaddhaṃ, saddhaṃ vaḍḍheti sāsane; Evaṃ me sutamiccevaṃ, vadaṃ gotamasāvako’’ti. Ekanti gaṇanaparicchedaniddeso. Samayanti paricchinnaniddeso. Ekaṃ samayanti aniyamitaparidīpanaṃ. Tattha samayasaddo – Eka Samaya Ekaṃ samaya samaya Samavāye khaṇe kāle, samūhe hetudiṭṭhisu; Paṭilābhe pahāne ca, paṭivedhe ca dissati. Tathā hissa ‘‘appeva nāma svepi upasaṅkameyyāma kālañca samayañca upādāyā’’ti evamādīsu (dī. ni. 1.447) samavāyo attho. ‘‘Ekova kho, bhikkhave, khaṇo ca samayo ca brahmacariyavāsāyā’’tiādīsu (a. ni. 8.29) khaṇo. ‘‘Uṇhasamayo pariḷāhasamayo’’tiādīsu (pāci. 358) kālo. ‘‘Mahāsamayo pavanasmi’’ntiādīsu samūho. ‘‘Samayopi kho te, bhaddāli, appaṭividdho ahosi, bhagavā kho sāvatthiyaṃ viharati, bhagavāpi maṃ jānissati, ‘bhaddāli, nāma bhikkhu satthusāsane sikkhāya na paripūrakārī’ti, ayampi kho te, bhaddāli, samayo appaṭividdho ahosī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 2.135) hetu . ‘‘Tena kho pana samayena uggāhamāno paribbājako samaṇamuṇḍikāputto samayappavādake tindukācīre ekasālake mallikāya ārāme paṭivasatī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 2.260) diṭṭhi. ‘‘Diṭṭhe dhamme ca yo attho, yo cattho samparāyiko; Atthābhisamayā dhīro, paṇḍitoti pavuccatī’’ti. – Ādīsu (saṃ. ni. 1.129) paṭilābho. ‘‘Sammā mānābhisamayā antamakāsi dukkhassā’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.24) pahānaṃ. ‘‘Dukkhassa pīḷanaṭṭho saṅkhataṭṭho santāpaṭṭho vipariṇāmaṭṭho abhisamayaṭṭho’’tiādīsu (paṭi. ma. 3.1) paṭivedho. Idha panassa kālo attho. Tena saṃvacchara-utu-māsaḍḍhamāsa-ratti-diva-pubbaṇha-majjhanhika-sāyanha- paṭhamamajjhimapacchimayāma-muhuttādīsu kālappabhedabhūtesu samayesu ekaṃ samayanti dīpeti. Tattha kiñcāpi etesu saṃvaccharādīsu samayesu yaṃ yaṃ suttaṃ yamhi yamhi saṃvacchare utumhi māse pakkhe rattibhāge divasabhāge vā vuttaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ therassa suviditaṃ suvavatthāpitaṃ paññāya. Yasmā pana ‘‘evaṃ me sutaṃ asukasaṃvacchare asukautumhi asukamāse asukapakkhe asukarattibhāge asukadivasabhāge vā’’ti evaṃ vutte na sakkā sukhena dhāretuṃ vā uddisituṃ vā uddisāpetuṃ vā, bahu ca vattabbaṃ hoti, tasmā ekeneva padena tamatthaṃ samodhānetvā ‘‘ekaṃ samaya’’nti āha. Ye vā ime gabbhokkantisamayo jātisamayo saṃvegasamayo abhinikkhamanasamayo dukkarakārikasamayo māravijayasamayo abhisambodhisamayo diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārasamayo desanāsamayo parinibbānasamayoti evamādayo bhagavato devamanussesu ativiya suppakāsā anekakālappabhedā eva samayā, tesu samayesu desanāsamayasaṅkhātaṃ ekaṃ samayanti dīpeti. Yo cāyaṃ ñāṇakaruṇākiccasamayesu aruṇākiccasamayo, attahitaparahitapaṭipattisamayesu parahitapaṭipattisamayo, sannipatitānaṃ karaṇīyadvayasamayesu dhammikathāsamayo, desanāpaṭipattisamayesu desanāsamayo, tesupi samayesu aññataraṃ sandhāya ‘‘ekaṃ samaya’’nti āha. Kasmā panettha yathā abhidhamme ‘‘yasmiṃ samaye kāmāvacara’’nti ca ito aññesu suttapadesu ‘‘yasmiṃ samaye, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehī’’ti ca bhummavacanena niddeso kato, vinaye ca ‘‘tena samayena buddho bhagavā’’ti karaṇavacanena, tathā akatvā ‘‘ekaṃ samaya’’nti upayogavacananiddeso katoti. Tattha tathā idha ca aññathā atthasambhavato. Tattha hi abhidhamme ito aññesu suttapadesu ca adhikaraṇattho bhāvenabhāvalakkhaṇattho ca sambhavati. Adhikaraṇañhi kālattho ca samūhattho ca samayo, tattha vuttānaṃ phassādidhammānaṃ khaṇasamavāyahetusaṅkhātassa ca samayassa bhāvena tesaṃ bhāvo lakkhīyati, tasmā tadatthajotanatthaṃ tattha bhummavacananiddeso kato. Vinaye ca hetuattho karaṇattho ca sambhavati. Yo hi so sikkhāpadapaññattisamayo sāriputtādīhipi dubbiññeyyo, tena samayena hetubhūtena karaṇabhūtena ca sikkhāpadāni paññāpayanto sikkhāpadapaññattihetuñca apekkhamāno bhagavā tattha tattha vihāsi, tasmā tadatthajotanatthaṃ tattha karaṇavacanena niddeso kato. Idha pana aññasmiñca evaṃjātike accantasaṃyogattho sambhavati. Yañhi samayaṃ bhagavā imaṃ aññaṃ vā suttantaṃ desesi, accantameva taṃ samayaṃ karuṇāvihārena vihāsi, tasmā tadatthajotanatthaṃ idha upayogavacananiddeso katoti. Tenetaṃ vuccati – ‘‘Taṃ taṃ atthamapekkhitvā, bhummena karaṇena ca; Aññatra samayo vutto, upayogena so idhā’’ti. Porāṇā pana vaṇṇayanti – ‘‘tasmiṃ samaye’’ti vā – ‘‘tena samayenā’’ti vā – ‘‘ekaṃ samaya’’nti vā abhilāpamattabhedo esa, sabbattha bhummameva atthoti. Tasmā ‘‘ekaṃ samaya’’nti vuttepi ‘‘ekasmiṃ samaye’’ti attho veditabbo. Bhagavāti garu. Garuñhi loke ‘‘bhagavā’’ti vadanti. Ayañca sabbaguṇavisiṭṭhatāya sabbasattānaṃ garu, tasmā ‘‘bhagavā’’ti veditabbo. Porāṇehipi vuttaṃ – Bhagavā ‘‘Bhagavāti vacanaṃ seṭṭhaṃ, bhagavāti vacanamuttamaṃ; Garugāravayutto so, bhagavā tena vuccatī’’ti. Apica – ‘‘Bhāgyavā bhaggavā yutto, bhagehi ca vibhattavā; Bhattavā vantagamano, bhavesu bhagavā tato’’ti. – Imissā gāthāya vasenassa padassa vitthārato attho veditabbo. So ca visuddhimagge buddhānussatiniddese vuttoyeva. visuddhimagge Ettāvatā cettha evaṃ me sutanti vacanena yathāsutaṃ dhammaṃ dassento bhagavato dhammasarīraṃ paccakkhaṃ karoti. Tena – ‘‘nayidaṃ atikkantasatthukaṃ pāvacanaṃ, ayaṃ vo satthā’’ti satthu adassanena ukkaṇṭhitaṃ janaṃ samassāseti. evaṃ me suta Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavāti vacanena tasmiṃ samaye bhagavato avijjamānabhāvaṃ dassento rūpakāyaparinibbānaṃ sādheti. Tena ‘‘evaṃvidhassa nāma ariyadhammassa desako dasabaladharo vajirasaṅghātasamānakāyo, sopi bhagavā parinibbuto, kena aññena jīvite āsā janetabbā’’ti jīvitamadamattaṃ janaṃ saṃvejeti, saddhamme cassa ussāhaṃ janeti. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā Evanti ca bhaṇanto desanāsampattiṃ niddisati. Me sutanti sāvakasampattiṃ. Ekaṃ samayanti kālasampattiṃ. Bhagavāti desakasampattiṃ. Eva Me suta Ekaṃ samaya Bhagavā Ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharatīti ettha ukkāti dīpikā, tañca nagaraṃ ‘‘maṅgaladivaso sukhaṇo sunakkhattaṃ mā atikkamī’’ti rattimpi ukkāsu ṭhitāsu māpitattā ukkaṭṭhāti vuccati. Daṇḍadīpikāsu jāletvā dhārīyamānāsu māpitattāti vuttaṃ hoti, tassaṃ ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ. Samīpatthe cetaṃ bhummavacanaṃ. Viharatīti avisesena iriyāpathadibbabrahmaariyavihāresu aññataravihārasamaṅgiparidīpanametaṃ. Idha pana ṭhānagamananisinnasayanappabhedesu riyāpathesu aññatarairiyāpathasamāyogaparidīpanaṃ. Tena ṭhitopi gacchantopi nisinnopi sayānopi bhagavā viharaticceva veditabbo. So hi bhagavā ekaṃ iriyāpathabādhanaṃ aññena iriyāpathena vicchinditvā aparipatantaṃ attabhāvaṃ harati pavatteti, tasmā viharatīti vuccati. ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ Viharatī Subhagavaneti ettha subhagattā subhagaṃ, sundarasirikattā sundarakāmattā cāti vuttaṃ hoti. Tassa hi vanassa sirisampattiyā manussā annapānādīni ādāya divasaṃ tattheva chaṇasamajjaussave karontā bhogasukhaṃ anubhonti, sundarasundare cettha kāme patthenti ‘‘puttaṃ labhāma, dhītaraṃ labhāmā’’ti, tesaṃ taṃ tatheva hoti, evaṃ taṃ sundarasirikattā sundarakāmattā ca subhagaṃ. Apica bahujanakantatāyapi subhagaṃ. Vanayatīti vanaṃ, attasampadāya sattānaṃ bhattiṃ kāreti, attani sinehaṃ uppādetīti attho. Vanute iti vā vanaṃ, nānāvidhakusuma-gandhasammodamattakokilādivihaṅgamābhirutehi mandamālutacalitarukkhasākhāviṭapapallavapalāsehi ca ‘‘etha maṃ paribhuñjathā’’ti sabbapāṇino yācati viyāti attho. Subhagañca taṃ vanañcāti subhagavanaṃ. Tasmiṃ subhagavane. Vanañca nāma ropimaṃ, sayaṃjātanti duvidhaṃ. Tattha veḷuvanajetavanādīni ropimāni. Andhavanamahāvanaañjanavanādīni sayaṃ jātāni. Idampi sayaṃjātanti veditabbaṃ. Subhagavane subhagaṃ, subhagaṃ vanaṃ, vanaṃ, subhagavanaṃ subhagavane Sālarājamūleti ettha sālarukkhopi sāloti vuccati. Yathāha ‘‘seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, gāmassa vā nigamassa vā avidūre mahantaṃ sālavanaṃ, tañcassa eḷaṇḍehi sañchanna’’nti (ma. ni. 1.225) ‘‘antarena yamakasālāna’’nti ca (dī. ni. 2.195) vanappatijeṭṭhakarukkhopi. Yathāha – Sālarājamūle ‘‘Taveva deva vijite, tavevuyyānabhūmiyā; Ujuvaṃsā mahāsālā, nīlobhāsā manoramā’’ti. (jā. 2.19.4); Yo koci rukkhopi. Yathāha ‘‘atha kho taṃ, bhikkhave, māluvabījaṃ aññatarasmiṃ sālamūle nipateyyā’’ti (ma. ni. 1.469). Idha pana vanappatijeṭṭhakarukkho adhippeto. Rājasaddo panassa tameva jeṭṭhakabhāvaṃ sādheti. Yathāha ‘‘suppatiṭṭhitassa kho brāhmaṇa dhammika nigrodharājassā’’ti (a. ni. 6.54). Tattha dvedhā samāso, sālānaṃ rājātipi sālarājā, sālo ca so jeṭṭhakaṭṭhena rājā ca itipi sālarājā. Mūlanti samīpaṃ. Ayañhi mūlasaddo, ‘‘mūlāni uddhareyya, antamaso usiranāḷimattānipī’’tiādīsu (a. ni. 4.195) mūlamūle dissati. ‘‘Lobho akusalamūla’’ntiādīsu (dī. ni. 3.305) asādhāraṇahetumhi. ‘‘Yāva majjhanhike kāle chāyā pharati, nivāte paṇṇāni patanti, ettāvatā rukkhamūla’’ntiādīsu samīpe. Idha pana samīpe adhippeto, tasmā sālarājassa samīpeti evamettha attho daṭṭhabbo. sālarājā, sālarājā. Mūla Tattha siyā – yadi tāva bhagavā ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharati, ‘‘subhagavane sālarājamūle’’ti na vattabbaṃ, atha tattha viharati, ‘‘ukkaṭṭhāya’’nti na vattabbaṃ, na hi sakkā ubhayattha ekaṃ samayaṃ viharitunti. Na kho panetaṃ evaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Nanu avocumha ‘‘samīpatthe cetaṃ bhummavacana’’nti. Tasmā yathā gaṅgāyamunādīnaṃ samīpe goyūthāni carantāni ‘‘gaṅgāya caranti, yamunāya carantī’’ti vuccanti, evamidhāpi yadidaṃ ukkaṭṭhāya samīpe subhagavanaṃ sālarājamūlaṃ, tattha viharanto vuccati ‘‘ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharati subhagavane sālarājamūle’’ti. Gocaragāmanidassanatthañhissa ukkaṭṭhāvacanaṃ, pabbajitānurūpanivāsaṭṭhānanidassanatthaṃ sesavacanaṃ. Tattha ukkaṭṭhākittanena āyasmā ānando bhagavato gahaṭṭhānuggahakaraṇaṃ dasseti, subhagavanādikittanena pabbajitānuggahakaraṇaṃ. Tathā purimena paccayaggahaṇato attakilamathānuyogavivajjanaṃ, pacchimena vatthukāmappahānato kāmasukhallikānuyogavivajjanupāyadassanaṃ. Purimena ca dhammadesanābhiyogaṃ, pacchimena vivekādhimuttiṃ. Purimena karuṇāya upagamanaṃ, pacchimena paññāya apagamanaṃ. Purimena sattānaṃ hitasukhanipphādanādhimuttataṃ, pacchimena parahitasukhakaraṇe nirupalepanaṃ. Purimena dhammikasukhāpariccāganimittaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ, pacchimena uttarimanussadhammānuyoganimittaṃ . Purimena manussānaṃ upakārabahulataṃ, pacchimena devānaṃ. Purimena loke jātassa loke saṃvaḍḍhabhāvaṃ, pacchimena lokena anupalittataṃ. Purimena ‘‘ekapuggalo, bhikkhave, loke uppajjamāno uppajjati bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katamo ekapuggalo, tathāgato arahaṃ sammāsambuddho’’ti (a. ni. 1.170) vacanato yadatthaṃ bhagavā uppanno, tadatthaparinipphādanaṃ, pacchimena yattha uppanno, tadanurūpavihāraṃ. Bhagavā hi paṭhamaṃ lumbinivane, dutiyaṃ bodhimaṇḍeti lokiyalokuttarāya uppattiyā vaneyeva uppanno, tenassa vaneyeva vihāraṃ dassetīti evamādinā nayenettha atthayojanā veditabbā. Tatrāti desakālaparidīpanaṃ. Tañhi yaṃ samayaṃ viharati, tatra samaye. Yasmiñca sālarājamūle viharati, tatra sālarājamūleti dīpeti. Bhāsitabbayutte vā desakāle dīpeti. Na hi bhagavā ayutte dese kāle vā dhammaṃ bhāsati. ‘‘Akālo kho tāva bāhiyā’’ti (udā. 10) ādicettha sādhakaṃ. Khoti padapūraṇamatte avadhāraṇe ādikālatthe vā nipāto. Bhagavāti lokagarudīpanaṃ. Bhikkhūti kathāsavanayuttapuggalavacanaṃ. Apicettha, ‘‘bhikkhakoti bhikkhu, bhikkhācariyaṃ ajjhupagatoti bhikkhū’’tiādinā (pārā. 45) nayena vacanattho veditabbo. Āmantesīti ālapi abhāsi sambodhesīti ayamettha attho. Aññatra pana ñāpanepi hoti. Yathāha ‘‘āmantayāmi vo, bhikkhave, paṭivedayāmi vo, bhikkhave’’ti. Pakkosanepi. Yathāha ‘‘ehi tvaṃ bhikkhu mama vacanena sāriputtaṃ āmantehī’’ti (a. ni. 9.11). Tatrā Kho Bhagavā Bhikkhū Āmantesī Bhikkhavoti āmantanākāradīpanaṃ. Tañca bhikkhanasīlatādiguṇayogasiddhattā vuttaṃ. Bhikkhanasīlatāguṇayuttopi hi bhikkhu bhikkhanadhammatāguṇayuttopi. Bhikkhane sādhukāritāguṇayuttopīti saddavidū maññanti. Tena ca nesaṃ bhikkhanasīlatādiguṇayogasiddhena vacanena hīnādhikajanasevitaṃ vuttiṃ pakāsento uddhatadīnabhāvaniggahaṃ karoti. Bhikkhavoti iminā ca karuṇāvipphārasommahadayanayananipātapubbaṅgamena vacanena te attano mukhābhimukhe karoti. Teneva ca kathetukamyatādīpakena vacanena nesaṃ sotukamyataṃ janeti. Teneva ca sambodhanaṭṭhena sādhukaṃ savanamanasikārepi ne niyojeti. Sādhukasavanamanasikārāyattā hi sāsanasampatti. Bhikkhavo bhikkhu Aparesupi devamanussesu vijjamānesu kasmā bhikkhūyeva āmantesīti ce. Jeṭṭhaseṭṭhāsannasadāsannihitabhāvato. Sabbaparisasādhāraṇā hi bhagavato dhammadesanā. Parisāya ca jeṭṭhā bhikkhū, paṭhamuppannattā. Seṭṭhā, anagāriyabhāvaṃ ādiṃ katvā satthucariyānuvidhāyakattā sakalasāsanapaṭiggāhakattā ca. Āsannā, tattha nisinnesu satthusantikattā. Sadāsannihitā, satthusantikāvacarattāti. Apica te dhammadesanāya bhājanaṃ, yathānusiṭṭhaṃ paṭipattisabbhāvato. Visesato ca ekacce bhikkhūyeva sandhāya ayaṃ desanātipi te eva āmantesi. Tattha siyā – kimatthaṃ pana bhagavā dhammaṃ desento paṭhamaṃ bhikkhū āmantesi, na dhammameva desetīti. Satijananatthaṃ. Bhikkhū hi aññaṃ cintentāpi vikkhittacittāpi dhammaṃ paccavekkhantāpi kammaṭṭhānaṃ manasikarontāpi nisinnā honti, te anāmantetvā dhamme desiyamāne – ‘‘ayaṃ desanā kinnidānā kiṃpaccayā katamāya aṭṭhuppattiyā desitā’’ti sallakkhetuṃ asakkontā duggahitaṃ vā gaṇheyyuṃ, na vā gaṇheyyuṃ. Tena nesaṃ satijananatthaṃ bhagavā paṭhamaṃ āmantetvā pacchā dhammaṃ deseti. Bhadanteti gāravavacanametaṃ, satthuno paṭivacanadānaṃ vā, apicettha bhikkhavoti vadamāno bhagavā te bhikkhū ālapati. Bhadanteti vadamānā te bhagavantaṃ paccālapanti. Tathā bhikkhavoti bhagavā ābhāsati. Bhadanteti te paccābhāsanti. Bhikkhavoti paṭivacanaṃ dāpeti, bhadanteti paṭivacanaṃ denti. Te bhikkhūti ye bhagavā āmantesi. Bhagavato paccassosunti bhagavato āmantanaṃ paṭiassosuṃ, abhimukhā hutvā suṇiṃsu sampaṭicchiṃsu paṭiggahesunti attho. Bhagavā etadavocāti bhagavā etaṃ idāni vattabbaṃ sakalaṃ suttaṃ avoca. Bhadante Te bhikkhū Bhagavato paccassosu Bhagavā etadavocā Ettāvatā ca yaṃ āyasmatā ānandena kamalakuvalayujjalavimalasādurasasalilāya pokkharaṇiyā sukhāvataraṇatthaṃ nimmalasilātalaracanavilāsasobhitaratanasopānaṃ vippakiṇṇamuttātalasadisavālikākiṇṇapaṇḍarabhūmibhāgaṃ titthaṃ viya suvibhattabhittivicitravedikāparikkhittassa nakkhattapathaṃ phusitukāmatāya viya, vijambhitasamussayassa pāsādavarassa sukhārohaṇatthaṃ dantamaya-saṇhamuduphalaka-kañcanalatāvinaddha- maṇigaṇappabhāsamudayujjalasobhaṃ sopānaṃ viya, suvaṇṇavalayānūpurādisaṅghaṭṭanasaddasammissitakathitahasitamadhurassaragehajanavicaritassa uḷāraissariyavibhavasobhitassa mahāgharassa sukhappavesanatthaṃ suvaṇṇarajatamaṇimuttāpavāḷādijutivissaravijjotita-suppatiṭṭhitavisāladvārabāhaṃ mahādvāraṃ viya ca atthabyañjanasampannassa buddhānaṃ desanāñāṇagambhīrabhāvasaṃsūcakassa imassa suttassa sukhāvagāhaṇatthaṃ kāladesadesakavatthuparisāpadesapaṭimaṇḍitaṃ nidānaṃ bhāsitaṃ, tassa atthavaṇṇanā samattā. Suttanikkhepavaṇṇanā Idāni ‘‘sabbadhammamūlapariyāyaṃ vo’’tiādinā nayena bhagavatā nikkhittassa suttassa vaṇṇanāya okāso anuppatto. Sā panesā suttavaṇṇanā yasmā suttanikkhepaṃ vicāretvā vuccamānā pākaṭā hoti, tasmā suttanikkhepaṃ tāva vicārayissāma. Cattāro hi suttanikkhepā attajjhāsayo parajjhāsayo pucchāvasiko aṭṭhuppattikoti. Tattha yāni suttāni bhagavā parehi anajjhiṭṭho kevalaṃ attano ajjhāsayeneva kathesi. Seyyathidaṃ, ākaṅkheyyasuttaṃ, vatthasuttaṃ, mahāsatipaṭṭhānasuttaṃ, mahāsaḷāyatanavibhaṅgasuttaṃ, ariyavaṃsasuttaṃ, sammappadhānasuttantahārako, iddhipādaindriyabalabojjhaṅgamaggaṅgasuttantahārakoti evamādīni. Tesaṃ attajjhāsayo nikkhepo. Yāni pana ‘‘paripakkā kho rāhulassa vimuttiparipācanīyā dhammā, yaṃnūnāhaṃ rāhulaṃ uttari āsavānaṃ khaye vineyya’’nti (saṃ. ni. 4.121) evaṃ paresaṃ ajjhāsayaṃ khantiṃ manaṃ abhinīhāraṃ bujjhanabhāvañca avekkhitvā parajjhāsayavasena kathitāni. Seyyathidaṃ, cūḷarāhulovādasuttaṃ, mahārāhulovādasuttaṃ, dhammacakkappavattanaṃ, dhātuvibhaṅgasuttanti evamādīni. Tesaṃ parajjhāsayo nikkhepo. Bhagavantaṃ pana upasaṅkamitvā catasso parisā cattāro vaṇṇā nāgā supaṇṇā gandhabbā asurā yakkhā mahārājāno tāvatiṃsādayo devā mahābrahmāti evamādayo ‘‘bojjhaṅgā bojjhaṅgā’’ti, bhante, vuccanti. ‘‘Nīvaraṇā nīvaraṇā’’ti, bhante, vuccanti. Ime nu kho, bhante, pañcupādānakkhandhā. ‘‘Kiṃ sūdha vittaṃ purisassa seṭṭha’’ntiādinā (su. ni. 183) nayena pañhaṃ pucchanti. Evaṃ puṭṭhena bhagavatā yāni kathitāni bojjhaṅgasaṃyuttādīni. Yāni vā panaññānipi devatāsaṃyutta-mārasaṃyutta-brahmasaṃyutta-sakkapañha-cūḷavedalla-mahāvedalla-sāmaññaphala- āḷavaka-sūciloma-kharalomasuttādīni, tesaṃ pucchāvasiko nikkhepo. Yāni panetāni uppannaṃ kāraṇaṃ paṭicca kathitāni. Seyyathidaṃ, dhammadāyādaṃ cūḷasīhanādaṃ candūpamaṃ puttamaṃsūpamaṃ dārukkhandhūpamaṃ aggikkhandhūpamaṃ pheṇapiṇḍūpamaṃ pāricchattakūpamanti evamādīni. Tesaṃ aṭṭhuppattiko nikkhepo. Evamimesu catūsu nikkhepesu imassa suttassa aṭṭhuppattiko nikkhepo. Aṭṭhuppattiyañhi idaṃ bhagavatā nikkhittaṃ. Katarāya aṭṭhuppattiyā? Pariyattiṃ nissāya uppanne māne. Pañcasatā kira brāhmaṇā tiṇṇaṃ vedānaṃ pāragū aparabhāge bhagavato dhammadesanaṃ sutvā kāmesu ādīnavaṃ nekkhamme ca ānisaṃsaṃ sampassamānā bhagavato santike pabbajitvā nacirasseva sabbaṃ buddhavacanaṃ uggaṇhitvā pariyattiṃ nissāya mānaṃ uppādesuṃ ‘‘yaṃ yaṃ bhagavā katheti, taṃ taṃ mayaṃ khippameva jānāma, bhagavā hi tīṇi liṅgāni cattāri padāni satta vibhattiyo muñcitvā na kiñci katheti, evaṃ kathite ca amhākaṃ gaṇṭhipadaṃ nāma natthī’’ti. Te bhagavati agāravā hutvā tato paṭṭhāya bhagavato upaṭṭhānampi dhammassavanampi abhiṇhaṃ na gacchanti. Bhagavā tesaṃ taṃ cittacāraṃ ñatvā ‘‘abhabbā ime imaṃ mānakhilaṃ anupahacca maggaṃ vā phalaṃ vā sacchikātu’’nti tesaṃ sutapariyattiṃ nissāya uppannaṃ mānaṃ aṭṭhuppattiṃ katvā desanākusalo bhagavā mānabhañjanatthaṃ sabbadhammamūlapariyāyanti desanaṃ ārabhi. sabbadhammamūlapariyāya Tattha sabbadhammamūlapariyāyanti sabbesaṃ dhammānaṃ mūlapariyāyaṃ. Sabbesanti anavasesānaṃ. Anavasesavācako hi ayaṃ sabba-saddo. So yena yena sambandhaṃ gacchati, tassa tassa anavasesataṃ dīpeti. Yathā, ‘‘sabbaṃ rūpaṃ aniccaṃ sabbā vedanā aniccā sabbasakkāyapariyāpannesu dhammesū’’ti. Dhamma-saddo panāyaṃ pariyatti-sacca-samādhi-paññā-pakati-sabhāvasuññatā-puññāpatti-ñeyyādīsu dissati. ‘‘Idha bhikkhu dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇāti suttaṃ geyya’’ntiādīsu (a. ni. 5.73) hi dhammasaddo pariyattiyaṃ vattati. ‘‘Diṭṭhadhammo viditadhammo’’tiādīsu (dī. ni. 1.299) saccesu. ‘‘Evaṃ dhammā te bhagavanto’’tiādīsu samādhimhi. sabbadhammamūlapariyāya sabba Dhamma ‘‘Yassete caturo dhammā, vānarinda yathā tava; Saccaṃ dhammo dhiti cāgo, diṭṭhaṃ so ativattatī’’ti. – Ādīsu (jā. 1.1.57) paññāya. ‘‘Jātidhammā jarādhammā, atho maraṇadhammino’’tiādīsu pakatiyaṃ. ‘‘Kusalā dhammā’’tiādīsu (dha. sa. 1.tikamātikā) sabhāve. ‘‘Tasmiṃ kho pana samaye dhammā hontī’’tiādīsu (dha. sa. 121) suññatāyaṃ. ‘‘Dhammo suciṇṇo sukhamāvahātī’’tiādīsu (jā. 1.10.102) puññe. ‘‘Dve aniyatā dhammā’’tiādīsu (pārā. 443) āpattiyaṃ. ‘‘Sabbe dhammā sabbākārena buddhassa bhagavato ñāṇamukhe āpāthaṃ āgacchantī’’tiādīsu ñeyye. Idha panāyaṃ sabhāve vattati. Tatrāyaṃ vacanattho – attano lakkhaṇaṃ dhārentīti dhammā. Mūla-saddo vitthārito eva. Idha panāyaṃ asādhāraṇahetumhi daṭṭhabbo. dhammā. Mūla Pariyāyasaddo ‘‘madhupiṇḍikapariyāyoti naṃ dhārehī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.205) desanāyaṃ vattati. ‘‘Atthi khvesa brāhmaṇa, pariyāyo, yena maṃ pariyāyena sammā vadamāno vadeyya akiriyavādo samaṇo gotamo’’tiādīsu (pārā. 3) kāraṇe. ‘‘Kassa nu kho, ānanda, ajja pariyāyo bhikkhuniyo ovaditu’’ntiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.398) vāre. Idha pana kāraṇepi desanāyampi vattati. Tasmā ‘‘sabbadhammamūlapariyāya’’nti ettha sabbesaṃ dhammānaṃ asādhāraṇahetusaññitaṃ kāraṇanti vā sabbesaṃ dhammānaṃ kāraṇadesananti vā evaṃ attho daṭṭhabbo. Neyyatthattā cassa suttassa, na catubhūmakāpi sabhāvadhammā sabbadhammāti veditabbā. Sakkāyapariyāpannā pana tebhūmakā dhammāva anavasesato veditabbā, ayamettha adhippāyoti. Pariyāya Voti ayaṃ vo-saddo paccattaupayogakaraṇasampadānasāmivacanapadapūraṇesu dissati. ‘‘Kacci pana vo, anuruddhā, samaggā sammodamānā’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.326) hi paccatte dissati. ‘‘Gacchatha, bhikkhave, paṇāmemi vo’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 2.157) upayoge. ‘‘Na vo mama santike vatthabba’’ntiādīsu (ma. ni. 2.157) karaṇe. ‘‘Vanapatthapariyāyaṃ vo, bhikkhave, desessāmī’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.190) sampadāne. ‘‘Sabbesaṃ vo, sāriputta, subhāsita’’ntiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.345) sāmivacane. ‘‘Ye hi vo ariyā parisuddhakāyakammantā’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.35) padapūraṇamatte. Idha panāyaṃ sampadāne daṭṭhabbo. Vo Bhikkhaveti patissavena abhimukhībhūtānaṃ punālapanaṃ. Desessāmīti desanāpaṭijānanaṃ. Idaṃ vuttaṃ hoti, bhikkhave, sabbadhammānaṃ mūlakāraṇaṃ tumhākaṃ desessāmi, dutiyena nayena kāraṇadesanaṃ tumhākaṃ desessāmīti. Taṃ suṇāthāti tamatthaṃ taṃ kāraṇaṃ taṃ desanaṃ mayā vuccamānaṃ suṇātha. Sādhukaṃ manasi karothāti ettha pana sādhukaṃ sādhūti ekatthametaṃ. Ayañca sādhu saddo āyācanasampaṭicchanasampahaṃsanasundaradaḷhīkammādīsu dissati. ‘‘Sādhu me bhante bhagavā, saṃkhittena dhammaṃ desetū’’tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 4.95) hi āyācane dissati. ‘‘Sādhu, bhanteti kho so bhikkhu bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā’’tiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.86) sampaṭicchane. ‘‘Sādhu, sādhu sāriputtā’’tiādīsu (dī. ni. 3.349) sampahaṃsane. Bhikkhave Desessāmī Taṃ suṇāthā Sādhukaṃ manasi karothā ‘‘Sādhu dhammarucī rājā, sādhu paññāṇavā naro; Sādhu mittānamaddubbho, pāpassākaraṇaṃ sukha’’nti. Ādīsu (jā. 2.18.101) sundare. ‘‘Tena hi, brāhmaṇa, sādhukaṃ suṇāhī’’tiādīsu (a. ni. 5.192) sādhukasaddoyeva daḷhīkamme, āṇattiyantipi vuccati. Idhāpi ayaṃ ettheva daḷhīkamme ca āṇattiyañca attho veditabbo. Sundaratthepi vattati. Daḷhīkaraṇatthena hi daḷhamimaṃ dhammaṃ suṇātha suggahitaṃ gaṇhantā. Āṇattiatthena mama āṇattiyā suṇātha. Sundaratthena sundaramimaṃ bhaddakaṃ dhammaṃ suṇāthāti evaṃ dīpitaṃ hoti. Manasikarothāti āvajjetha, samannāharathāti attho, avikkhittacittā hutvā nisāmetha citte karothāti adhippāyo. Idānettha taṃ suṇāthāti sotindriyavikkhepavāraṇametaṃ. Sādhukaṃ manasi karothāti manasikāre daḷhīkammaniyojanena manindriyavikkhepavāraṇaṃ. Purimañcettha byañjanavipallāsaggāhavāraṇaṃ, pacchimaṃ atthavipallāsaggāhavāraṇaṃ. Purimena ca dhammassavane niyojeti, pacchimena sutānaṃ dhammānaṃ dhāraṇūpaparikkhādīsu. Purimena ca sabyañjano ayaṃ dhammo, tasmā savanīyoti dīpeti. Pacchimena sāttho, tasmā manasi kātabboti. Sādhukapadaṃ vā ubhayapadehi yojetvā yasmā ayaṃ dhammo dhammagambhīro desanāgambhīro ca, tasmā suṇātha sādhukaṃ, yasmā atthagambhīro paṭivedhagambhīro ca, tasmā sādhukaṃ manasi karothāti evaṃ yojanā veditabbā. Manasi karothā taṃ suṇāthā Bhāsissāmīti desessāmi. ‘‘Taṃ suṇāthā’’ti ettha paṭiññātaṃ desanaṃ na saṃkhittatova desessāmi, apica kho vitthāratopi naṃ bhāsissāmīti vuttaṃ hoti, saṅkhepavitthāravācakāni hi etāni padāni. Yathāha vaṅgīsatthero – Bhāsissāmī ‘‘Saṃkhittenapi deseti, vitthārenapi bhāsati; Sāḷikāyiva nigghoso, paṭibhānaṃ udīrayī’’ti. (saṃ. ni. 1.214); Evaṃ vutte ussāhajātā hutvā evaṃ bhanteti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ satthu vacanaṃ sampaṭicchiṃsu, paṭiggahesunti vuttaṃ hoti. Atha nesaṃ bhagavā etadavoca etaṃ idāni vattabbaṃ idha bhikkhavotiādikaṃ sakalaṃ suttaṃ avoca. Tattha idhāti desāpadese nipāto. Svāyaṃ katthaci lokaṃ upādāya vuccati. Yathāha – ‘‘idha tathāgato loke uppajjatī’’ti (dī. ni. 1.190). Katthaci sāsanaṃ. Yathāha – ‘‘idheva, bhikkhave, samaṇo, idha dutiyo samaṇo’’ti (a. ni. 4.241). Katthaci okāsaṃ. Yathāha – evaṃ bhanteti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ bhagavā etadavoca idha bhikkhavo idhā ‘‘Idheva tiṭṭhamānassa, devabhūtassa me sato; Punarāyu ca me laddho, evaṃ jānāhi mārisā’’ti. (dī. ni. 2.369); Katthaci padapūraṇamattameva. Yathāha ‘‘idhāhaṃ – bhikkhave, bhuttāvī assaṃ pavārito’’ti (ma. ni. 1.30). Idha pana lokaṃ upādāya vuttoti veditabbo.
1. This is the introductory nidāna beginning with "Thus have I heard."

Here, evaṃ is a particle (nipātapadaṃ).

me etc. are nouns (nāmapadāni).

In "He dwells in a prominent place" (ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharatī), vi- is a prefix (upasaggapadaṃ), and haratī is a verb (ākhyātapadanti); in this way, the division of words should be understood.

In terms of meaning, however, the word evaṃ has various distinctions, such as simile, instruction, praise, reproof, acceptance of a statement, mode, example, and ascertainment.

Likewise, it appears as a simile in passages such as "Much profitable action can by one thus mortal born be done" (evaṃ jātena maccena kattabbaṃ kusalaṃ bahu).

It appears as giving directions in passages such as "You should move forward thus and move backward thus" (evaṃ te abhikkamitabbaṃ, evaṃ te paṭikkamitabbaṃ).

It appears as praise in passages such as “‘So it is, Blessed One, so it is, Sublime One’" (evametaṃ bhagavā, evametaṃ sugatā).

It appears as reproof in passages such as "This is the way that this miserable woman speaks praise of that shaveling monk at every opportunity" (evamevaṃ panāyaṃ vasalī yasmiṃ vā tasmiṃ vā tassa muṇḍakassa samaṇakassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsatī).

It appears as acceptance of a statement in passages such as "‘Even so, venerable sir,’ the bhikkhus replied to the Blessed One" (evaṃ bhanteti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosu).

It appears as a mode in passages such as "'Precisely thus, venerable sir, do I understand the True Idea to be taught by the Blessed One'" (evaṃ byākho ahaṃ, bhante, bhagavatā dhammaṃ desitaṃ ājānāmī).

It appears as an example in passages such as "‘Come, young man, go to the samaṇa Ānanda; having gone, ask the samaṇa Ānanda in my words whether he is free from illness, free from disease, quick to rise, strong, and living in comfort: “Subha, the son of Todeyya, asks the honorable Ānanda whether you are free from illness, free from disease, quick to rise, strong, and living in comfort”; and say this: “It is said that the honorable Ānanda should go to the dwelling of Subha, the young son of Todeyya, out of compassion, taking it into consideration.”’" (ehi tvaṃ, māṇavaka, yena samaṇo ānando tenupasaṅkama, upasaṅkamitvā mama vacanena samaṇaṃ, ānandaṃ, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ puccha – ‘subho māṇavo todeyyaputto, bhavantaṃ ānandaṃ, appābādhaṃ appātaṅkaṃ lahuṭṭhānaṃ balaṃ phāsuvihāraṃ pucchatī’ti, evañca vadehi sādhu kira bhavaṃ ānando yena subhassa māṇavassa todeyyaputtassa nivesanaṃ, tenupasaṅkamatu anukampaṃ upādāyā).

"What do you think, Kālāmas, are these qualities wholesome or unwholesome?" (taṃ kiṃ maññatha, kālāmā, ime dhammā kusalā vā akusalā)..."Unwholesome, venerable sir." (akusalā, bhante).

"Blameworthy or blameless?" (sāvajjā vā anavajjā vāti)..."Blameworthy, venerable sir." (sāvajjā, bhante).

"Condemned or commended by the wise?" (viññugarahitā vā viññuppasatthā vāti)..."Condemned by the wise, venerable sir." (viññugarahitā, bhante).
Being undertaken and put into effect, do they lead to harm and suffering, or not, or how does it appear in this case? Venerable sir, being undertaken and put into effect, they lead to harm and suffering; that is how it appears to us in this case,’ etc. (A.
N.
3.
66) is for emphasis.
This should be understood here in the senses of mode, illustration, and emphasis.

Therein, in the sense of mode, the word evaṃ (thus) illustrates this meaning: Whose word is that Blessed One, who is expert in many methods, originating from various inclinations, perfect in meaning and letter, possessing various marvels, profound in teaching and penetrating ideas and meanings, suitable to all beings, each in his own speech, as soon as it comes to their ears? However, having generated a desire to hear in every way, [one says,] "Thus I heard; even I heard it in one way."

In the sense of illustration, by freeing himself, saying, "I am not self-born, nor have I realized this myself," he illustrates the entire sutta, which should now be said, "Thus I heard; even I heard it thus."

In the sense of emphasis, by showing his own power of remembering, corresponding to the praised qualities by the Blessed One thus, "Bhikkhus, foremost among the bhikkhus, my disciples, who are well-versed is Ānanda... who are mindful... who are firm... who are attendants is Ānanda" (A.
N.
1.
219-223), and by the Dhamma-commander thus, "The venerable Ānanda is skilled in meaning, skilled in Dhamma, skilled in letters, skilled in etymology, skilled in what precedes and what follows" (A.
N.
5.
169), he generates a desire to hear in beings, saying, "Thus I heard; and that, indeed, is neither less nor more in meaning or letter; it should be regarded just thus, not otherwise."

The word me (by me, to me, my) is seen in three meanings.
For in such passages as, “What has been earned by stanzas is uneatable by me” etc. (Sn.
81), the meaning is mayā (by me).
In such passages as, “It would be good, venerable sir, if the Blessed One would teach me the Dhamma in brief” etc. (S.
N.
4.
88), the meaning is mayhaṃ (to me).
In such passages as, “Bhikkhus, be my heirs in the True Idea” etc. (M.
N.
1.
29), the meaning is mama (my).
Here, however, both meanings, "by me heard" and "my heard experience," are appropriate.

As to the word sutaṃ (heard): this word suta, with and without prefixes, has many different meanings, such as going, celebrated, wet, heaped up, devotion to, cognizable by the ear, cognized through the ear-door, etc.
For in such passages as, “Proceeding with an army,” the meaning is gacchanto (going).
In such passages as, “Of one who sees the heard Dhamma” (Ud.
11), the meaning is vissutadhammassa (of the celebrated Dhamma).
In such passages as, “Dripping with the dripping one,” (Pāci.
657) the meaning is kilinnā kilinnassa (wet with the wet one).
In such passages as, "And how great merit can be stored away by you" etc.
7.12) upacitanti means increased.

In such passages as ‘Those steadfast ones intent on jhāna’ (Dhammapada 181), the meaning is devoted to jhāna.

In such passages as ‘Seen, heard, sensed’ (Majjhima Nikāya 1.241), the meaning is to be known by ear-consciousness.

In such passages as ‘He remembers what he has heard, stores up what he has heard’ (Majjhima Nikāya 1.339), the meaning is: one who bears in mind what is cognized in the ear-door.

Here, however, according to what is cognized in the ear-door, the meaning is either "ascertained" (upadhārita) or "ascertainment" (upadhāraṇa).

For when me means "by me" (mayā), it is fitting to say "Thus it was heard by me, ascertained according to what was cognized in the ear-door."

When mama means "my," it is fitting to say "Thus my hearing [was] an ascertainment according to what was cognized in the ear-door."

In these three terms, evaṃ indicates the function of consciousness, such as ear-consciousness, etc.;

me indicates the person who possesses the aforementioned consciousness;

sutaṃ indicates that there is no deficiency, excess, or perversion in what is apprehended, because the possibility of non-hearing is rejected.

Likewise, evaṃ expresses the fact that the stream of consciousness that occurs according to what is cognized in the ear-door occurs towards an object in various ways.

me is a declaration of self.

sutaṃ is a declaration of the Dharma.

Here, in brief, it is [as if one were saying]: "I have done nothing other than this with the stream of consciousness that occurs towards an object in various ways; however, this was done: this Dharma was heard."

Likewise, evaṃ is the indication of what should be pointed out.

me is the indication of the person.

sutaṃ is the indication of the person's action.

This is what was said: "That which I will point out as what has been heard (suttaṃ), that was thus heard by me."

Likewise, evaṃ is an indication of the various modes of the stream of consciousness of one whose mental continuum involves the apprehension of various meanings and expressions.

For here, evaṃ is a designation of mode (ākāra-paññatti), me is a designation of the agent (kattu-niddeśa), sutaṃ is a designation of the object (visaya-niddeśa).

Thus, by the mental continuum that occurs in various modes, the conclusion about the apprehension of the agent and the object associated with it is made.

Or, evaṃ is an indication of a person's action.

sutaṃ is an indication of the function of consciousness.

me is an indication of the person who possesses both functions.
Here, in brief, is the meaning: "Thus, it was heard by me," that is, by a person endowed with the faculty of hearing, through the act of consciousness, by means of the expression 'the act of hearing has been obtained'.

There, the evaṃ "thus" and me "by me" are designations that do not exist in the ultimate sense of reality.

For what is there here in the ultimate sense that could receive the designation "thus" or "by me"? Sutaṃ "heard" is an existing designation.

For what is apprehended here by the ear exists in the ultimate sense.

Likewise, the evaṃ "thus" and me "by me" are designations dependent on this or that, hence designations of dependence.

Sutaṃ "heard" is a designation based on what is stated with reference to what is seen, etc., hence a designation based on reference.

And here, by the word evaṃ "thus," he indicates non-delusion.

For a deluded person is not capable of various kinds of penetration.

By the word sutaṃ "heard," he indicates the non-confusion of what has been heard.

For someone whose hearing is confused does not acknowledge after a long time, "It was heard by me."

Through non-delusion, there is the attainment of wisdom; through non-confusion, however, there is the attainment of mindfulness.

There, wisdom, preceded by mindfulness, is capable of determining the meaning; mindfulness, preceded by wisdom, is capable of penetrating the meaning; by the combination of both capabilities, there is the attainment of the ability to maintain the treasury of the Dhamma, which is complete in meaning and expression, and thus the attainment of being a treasurer of the Dhamma.

Another way: By the word evaṃ "thus," he indicates reasoned attention, for there is no penetration of various kinds for one who does not attend in a reasoned way.

By the word sutaṃ "heard," he indicates non-distraction, for there is no hearing for one whose mind is distracted.

For when a distracted person is told quite unmistakably, he says, "I did not hear; say it again."

And by reasoned attention, he establishes the pair of blessings, namely, right self-direction and previously performed merit, since these are absent in one who is wrongly directed or has not performed merit previously.

By non-distraction, however, he establishes the hearing of the true Dhamma and reliance on true men.

For a distracted person is not able to hear, and there is no hearing for one who does not rely on true men.

Another way: Because the word evaṃ "thus" indicates that the mind-stream, with its various activities, grasps various meanings and expressions, it is said to have various designations.

And such a good quality is not present in one who is not rightly directed or has not performed merit previously; therefore, by this good quality, the word evaṃ "thus" indicates his attainment of the latter pair of wheels, and sutaṃ "heard" indicates the attainment of the former pair of wheels through the practice of hearing.

For there is no hearing for one who lives in an unsuitable place or lacks reliance on true men.

By the attainment of the latter pair of wheels, purity of inclination is established.

By the attainment of the former pair of wheels, purity of practice is established.

And by the purity of inclination, the attainment of distinction in realization is established; by the purity of practice, the attainment of distinction in scripture is established.

Thus, the word of one who is pure in practice and inclination, and who is accomplished in scripture and realization, is like the dawn arising before the sun, and like reasoned attention preceding skillful action, it is fitting to be the forerunner of the Blessed One's word, and in its place, establishing the source, he said, "Thus I have heard," and so on.

Another way: By this word evaṃ "thus," which indicates various kinds of penetration, he indicates the presence of his accomplishment in the qualities of spontaneous meaning and analytical insight.

By this word sutaṃ "heard," which indicates the penetration of the distinctions of what is to be heard, he indicates the presence of his accomplishment in the qualities of knowledge of the Dhamma and analytical insight.

And in uttering this word evaṃ "thus," which illuminates reasoned attention, he indicates, "These ideas have been looked over by the mind and properly penetrated by right view."

In uttering this word sutaṃ "heard," which illuminates the practice of hearing, he indicates, "Many ideas have been heard, remembered, and consolidated by word."

In showing by both words the completeness of the meaning and the letter, he inspires reverence in hearing.

For by not listening with reverence to the Dhamma that is complete in meaning and expression, one is excluded from great benefit, and thus, having inspired reverence, the Dhamma should be heard respectfully.
suta.
eva suta. “Thus have I heard (evaṃ me suta)” With this entire statement, venerable Ānanda, not attributing to himself the Dharma proclaimed by the Tathāgata, transcends the state of a bad person, and while acknowledging discipleship, enters into the state of a good person.
Similarly, he has his cognizance emerge from what is not the True Idea (asaddhammā cittaṃ vuṭṭhāpeti) and establishes it upon the True Idea (saddhamme cittaṃ patiṭṭhāpeti).
“All this is only heard by me (kevalaṃ sutamevetaṃ mayā), but it is the word of the Blessed One himself (tasseva pana bhagavato vacana)” In showing that, he effaces himself and bears witness to the Master (satthāraṃ apadisati), he fixes the Victor’s words (jinavacanaṃ appeti) and establishes the True Idea as the guide (dhammanettiṃ patiṭṭhāpeti).

Moreover, “Thus have I heard (evaṃ me suta)” not acknowledging a state produced by himself, explaining the former statement, “This I have received in person from the Blessed One (sammukhā paṭiggahitamidaṃ mayā tassa bhagavato), who is skilled in the four kinds of self-confidence (catuvesārajjavisāradassa), possesses the ten powers (dasabaladharassa), stands in the place of the bull (āsabhaṭṭhānaṭṭhāyino), roars the lion’s roar (sīhanādanādino), is the best of all beings (sabbasattuttamassa), the lord of the Dhamma (dhammissarassa), the king of the Dhamma (dhammarājassa), the sovereign of the Dhamma (dhammādhipatino), the lamp of the Dhamma (dhammadīpassa), the refuge of the Dhamma (dhammasaraṇassa), the noble wheel-turner of the True Dhamma (saddhammavaracakkavattino), the perfectly enlightened one (sammāsambuddhassa), there should be no doubt or uncertainty about the meaning or the Dharma or the word or the expression (na ettha atthe vā dhamme vā pade vā byañjane vā kaṅkhā vā vimati vā kattabbā)” in this Dhamma, he destroys disbelief in all gods and humans (sabbadevamanussānaṃ imasmiṃ dhamme assaddhiyaṃ vināseti), he generates the accomplishment of faith (saddhāsampadaṃ uppādetīti).
Therefore it is said:

“He destroys disbelief (vināsayati assaddhaṃ), he increases faith in the Dispensation (saddhaṃ vaḍḍheti sāsane);
Thus I have heard (evaṃ me sutamiccevaṃ), the disciple of Gotama declares (vadaṃ gotamasāvako’'ti)."

eka (one) means a specification by counting (gaṇanaparicchedaniddeso).
samaya (occasion) means a defined specification (paricchinnaniddeso).
ekaṃ samaya (on one occasion) means an unspecified clarification (aniyamitaparidīpanaṃ).
There, the word samaya (occasion) –

eka (one) samaya (occasion), ekaṃ samaya (on one occasion), samaya (occasion): In the coming together (samavāye), the moment (khaṇe), the time (kāle), the mass (samūhe), the causes and views (hetudiṭṭhisu);

In gain (paṭilābhe) and abandonment (pahāne), and it is seen in penetration (paṭivedhe ca dissati).
tathā hissa "Just maybe, we might approach tomorrow, taking into account the right time and opportunity (appeva nāma svepi upasaṅkameyyāma kālañca samayañca upādāyā)” in such passages (dī.

ādīsu (saṃ.
ni.
1. 129) it is obtainment ( paṭilābho).

In such passages as ‘ With complete attainment of understanding of conceit he has made an end of suffering ’ ( ‘‘sammā mānābhisamayā antamakāsi dukkhassā’’tiādīsu pahānaṃ) it is abandonment ( pahānaṃ).

In such passages as ‘ Suffering’s meaning of oppression, meaning of being determined, meaning of torment, meaning of change, is its meaning of attainment (abhisamaya) ’ ( ‘‘dukkhassa pīḷanaṭṭho saṅkhataṭṭho santāpaṭṭho vipariṇāmaṭṭho abhisamayaṭṭho’’tiādīsu paṭivedho) it is penetration (paṭivedho).

Here, however, its meaning is time ( kālo attho).

Hence with the words ekaṃ samayaṃ what is meant is: one occasion among the occasions counted as year, season, month, half-month, night-and-day, morning, noon, evening, first watch, middle watch, last watch, hour, and so on.

There, although in these occasions of year, season, month and so on, whatever sutra that was spoken in whichever year, season, month, fortnight, night-part or day-part, all that was well-known and well-established by the elder’s wisdom.

However, since when it is said, "Thus have I heard, in such and such a year, in such and such a season, in such and such a month, in such and such a fortnight, in such and such a night part, or in such and such a day part," it is not easy to remember, or to point out oneself, or to have someone else point out, and much has to be said, therefore, having summarized that meaning in one word, he said, "ekaṃ samayaṃ."

Or, the occasions of conception, the occasion of birth, the occasion of agitation, the occasion of renunciation, the occasion of difficult practices, the occasion of victory over Māra, the occasion of complete enlightenment, the occasion of dwelling in the bliss of the present Dharma, the occasion of teaching, the occasion of final nirvāṇa—these and other such occasions of the Blessed One, which are very well known among gods and humans, are indeed various divisions of time. Among these occasions, it indicates one occasion, counted as the occasion of teaching.

And also, of the occasions of knowledge and compassion, the occasion of compassionate action, of the occasions of benefit for oneself and benefit for others, the occasion of practice for the benefit of others, of the occasions for the two duties of those who have gathered, the occasion for dharma talk, of the occasions for teaching and practice, the occasion for teaching; in those occasions too, referring to one or another, he said, "ekaṃ samayaṃ."

Why here (idha), unlike in the Abhidhamma where it is "at the time when (yasmiṃ samaye) it is in the sphere of desire (kāmāvacara)," and in other sūtra passages where it is "at the time when, monks, a monk quite secluded from desires (yasmiṃ samaye, bhikkhave, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehī)," the locative case (bhummavacanena) is used, and unlike in the Vinaya where it is "at that time (tena samayena) the Buddha, the Blessed One (buddho bhagavā)," the instrumental case (karaṇavacanena) is used, is the accusative case (upayogavacanena) "ekaṃ samayaṃ" used?

There it is like that, but here it is different, because the meaning is possible.

There, in the Abhidhamma and in other sūtra passages, the meaning of location and the meaning of characterization of one substantive by another substantive are possible.

For the occasion is the time and the mass; the occasion is the name of the cause of the combination of moments; and the substantive essence of those ideas beginning with contact stated there is characterized by the substantive essence of the occasion; so the locative case is used there in order to illustrate that meaning.

And in the Vinaya, the meaning of cause and the meaning of instrument are possible.

For that occasion of the enactment of a training precept is difficult to know even for Sāriputta and others; the Blessed One, enacting the training precepts with that occasion as the cause and as the instrument, and expecting the cause of the enactment of the training precept, stayed there and there; so the instrumental case is used there in order to illustrate that meaning.

Here, however, and in other such Sutta passages as this, the meaning implied is that of direct governance.

For the Blessed One lived that occasion with an abiding of direct compassion, when he preached this or any other Sutta. So the accusative case is used here in order to illustrate that meaning.

Therefore, it is said:

"Having considered that particular meaning, and with the locative and the instrumental, elsewhere ‘occasion’ is stated; but here it is with the accusative."

The ancients, however, explain: "Whether it is 'at that time' or 'by that time' or 'ekaṃ samayaṃ', this is merely a difference in expression; everywhere the meaning is the locative."

Therefore, even when "ekaṃ samayaṃ" is said, the meaning "at one time" should be understood.

Bhagavā means revered.
Indeed, in the world, they call [him] "Blessed One" (bhagavān).

And since he is pre-eminent in all good qualities, he is revered by all beings; therefore, he should be known as the "Blessed One" (bhagavān).

The ancients also said:

The word "Blessed One" (bhagavāti) is the best, the word "Blessed One" (bhagavāti) is supreme;

He is endowed with reverence and respect, therefore he is called "Blessed One" (bhagavā).

Moreover:

"He is fortunate (bhāgyavā), he has broken [defilements] (bhaggavā), he is endowed (yutto), and he is distinct in his possessions (bhagehi ca vibhattavā);

He is a partaker (bhattavā), his going is free from craving (vantagamano), therefore he is the Blessed One (bhagavā) in existences (bhavesu)."

–

The meaning of this verse should be understood in detail, according to the words.

And this is indeed stated in the Buddhānussati section of the Visuddhimagga.

In the Visuddhimagga:

"Thus have I heard" (evaṃ me suta); by this statement, showing the Dharma as it was heard, he makes the Blessed One's Dharma-body (dharmasarīra) manifest.

Therefore – "This is not a teaching that has passed away from the Teacher; this is your Teacher" – by not seeing the Teacher, he consoles the people who are longing [for him].

"On one occasion the Blessed One" (ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā); by this statement, showing the Blessed One's state of non-existence at that time, he establishes the passing away into parinirvāṇa of the form-body (rūpakāya).

Therefore, "Such a one by name, a preacher of the noble Dharma, a bearer of the ten powers, whose body was like a mass of diamonds, even that Blessed One has passed away into parinirvāṇa; by whom else should hope be generated in life?" – he inspires a sense of urgency in people intoxicated with the vanity of life and arouses in them a desire for the true Dharma.

"On one occasion the Blessed One" (ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā) saying this, he indicates the accomplishment of the teaching (desanāsampattiṃ).

"Thus have I heard" (me suta), [indicates] the accomplishment of the disciples (sāvakasampattiṃ).

"On one occasion" (ekaṃ samaya), [indicates] the accomplishment of the time (kālasampattiṃ).

"Blessed One" (bhagavā), [indicates] the accomplishment of the teacher (desakasampattiṃ).

"Thus have I heard" (evaṃ me suta), "On one occasion" (ekaṃ samaya), "the Blessed One" (bhagavā), "was dwelling at Ukkaṭṭhā" (ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharatī); here, "ukka" means a torch, and that city is called Ukkaṭṭhā because, when torches were erected, it was measured so that "the auspicious day, the pleasant moment, the good constellation may not pass."

It has been said that it was measured when torches were lit and held on lampstands; in that Ukkaṭṭhā.

Here, in the vicinity, this is a description of the place.

"Was dwelling" (viharatī); without distinction, in the postures, in the divine abodes, in the noble abodes this is a description of the association with any one of the abodes.

Here, however, it signifies occupation with one or another of the postures among those classed as standing, walking, sitting, and lying down.

So it should be understood that the Blessed One was so abiding whether standing, walking, sitting, or lying down.

For that Blessed One, having interrupted the oppressiveness of one posture with another posture, maintains and continues the non-falling away of [his] own being, therefore it is called "dwelling" (viharati).

"Was dwelling at Ukkaṭṭhā" (ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharatī) "At Subhagavana" (subhagavane); here, because it is fortunate (subhagattā) it is "fortunate" (subhagaṃ); it has been said that it is because of its beautiful splendor (sundarasirikattā) and because of its beautiful desires (sundarakāmattā).

For there, because of the splendor and prosperity of that forest, people, taking food and drink etc., spend the day there enjoying festivals and gatherings, they experience the pleasure of enjoyment, and here they desire things that are very beautiful, "May we obtain a son, may we obtain a daughter," for them it is just so; thus it is "fortunate" (subhagaṃ) because of its beautiful splendor and because of its beautiful desires.

Moreover, it is "fortunate" (subhagaṃ) also because it is desired by many people (bahujanakantatāyapi).

It is what one would , thus it is a wood; it creates fondness in creatures for its own excellence; the meaning is that it arouses affection in them towards itself .

Or alternatively, it would , thus it is a wood; the meaning is that with cries of cuckoos and other air-borne creatures intoxicated by the profusion of scents and fragrance of many sorts of flowers, and with its canopy of tree branches, fruits and verdant foliage swaying in the gentle breeze, it is, as it were, begging all creatures to ‘Come and enjoy me’.

The fortunate one and that forest, thus [it is called] the Fortunate Forest (subhagavanaṃ).

In that Fortunate Forest (tasmiṃ subhagavane).

And a forest, by name, is of two kinds: planted and self-grown.

There, Veḷuvana, Jetavana etc. are planted.

Andhavana, Mahāvana, Añjanavana etc. are self-grown.

This too should be known as self-grown.

In the Fortunate Forest (subhagavane), fortunate (subhagaṃ), fortunate (subhagaṃ), forest (vanaṃ), forest (vanaṃ), Fortunate Forest (subhagavanaṃ), in the Fortunate Forest (subhagavane) bhagavato paccassosunti" means they responded to the Bhagavān's address; having turned towards him, they listened, accepted, and received it.

"bhagavā etadavocāti" means the Bhagavān spoke this entire sutta, which was now to be spoken.

bhadante
te bhikkhū
bhagavato paccassosu
bhagavā etadavocā

Up to this point, the introductory narrative spoken by the Venerable Ānanda is completed. The introduction, by mentioning the time, place, teacher, background story, assembly, and region, helps facilitate comprehension of this sutta, perfect in meaning and phrasing, illustrating the greatness of the spiritual power of the Buddhas.

This is like a pond whose waters are as clear, pure, and sweet as the lotus and water lily, with jeweled steps beautifully constructed with immaculate flagstones for easy access; like a riverbank strewn with immaculate sand resembling a field of scattered pearls, surrounded by well-defined walls and colorful platforms; like stairs made of ivory, with smooth, soft treads, and resplendent with the glow of gems interwoven with golden vines for easy ascent to the magnificent palace that soars as if desiring to touch the constellations; and like a great gate with well-established, expansive doorposts, illuminated by the manifold radiance of gold, silver, jewels, pearls, and coral, for easy entry into the great house adorned with the opulence and grandeur of the sounds of bracelets, anklets, and other ornaments mingling with the sweet voices, laughter, and conversations of the womenfolk. The explanation of the meaning of that preface, embellished with time, place, teacher, subject matter, and assembly, for easy immersion in this sutta that is rich in meaning and expression, revealing the profound nature of the Buddhas’ teaching wisdom, is complete.

Commentary on the Grounds for the Delivery of a Sutta

Now, the opportunity has arisen to comment on the sutta laid down by the Bhagavān with the words, "I will teach you the Root Discourse on All Things (sabbadhammamūlapariyāyaṃ vo)," and so forth.

Since this commentary will be clearer if we first examine the grounds on which the Exalted One delivers a sutta, we will deal with this matter first.

There are four grounds for the delivery of a sutta: personal inclination (attajjhāsayo), the inclinations of others (parajjhāsayo), the proposal of a question (pucchāvasiko), and the occurrence of a special incident (aṭṭhuppattikoti).

Among these, those suttas which the Bhagavān declares entirely through his own inclination, without being requested by others, have personal inclination as the ground for their delivery. Some examples of this class are the Ākaṅkheyya Sutta, the Vattha Sutta, the Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna Sutta, the Mahāsaḷāyatanavibhaṅga Sutta, the Ariyavaṃsa Sutta, and many suttas on the right endeavors, the bases of spiritual success, the faculties, powers, factors of enlightenment, and factors of the path.

But those suttas that are spoken after considering the inclination, patience, mind, aspiration, and potential for awakening of others, with the thought, "Rahul’s qualities conducive to the ripening of liberation are mature; why don’t I train Rāhula in the destruction of the remaining āsavas?” (Saṃ. Ni. 4.121)—these are declared due to the inclinations of others. Some examples of this class are the Cūḷarāhulovāda Sutta, the Mahārāhulovāda Sutta, the Dhammacakkappavattana, and the Dhātuvibhaṅga Sutta. These have the inclinations of others as the ground for their delivery.

When the four assemblies, the four classes, nāgas, supaṇṇas, gandhabbas, asuras, yakkhas, great kings, the gods of the Tāvatiṃsa heaven, Mahābrahmās, and so forth approach the Bhagavān and ask questions—"Are the enlightenment factors enlightenment factors, venerable sir?" "Are the hindrances hindrances, venerable sir?" "Are these the five aggregates subject to clinging, venerable sir?"—or ask a question in the manner of, “What here is a man’s best treasure?” (Su. Ni. 183), and so on, and the Bhagavān speaks a sutta in reply, such as those connected with the Factors of Enlightenment (Bojjhaṅga-saṃyutta), and so forth, or also other suttas, such as those connected with the Deities (Devatā-saṃyutta), Māra (Māra-saṃyutta), Brahmā (Brahmā-saṃyutta), the Questions of Sakka (Sakkapañha), the Cūḷavedalla, the Mahāvedalla, the Sāmaññaphala, the Āḷavaka, the Sūciloma, and the Kharaloma Suttas, these have the proposal of a question as the ground for their delivery.

And those suttas declared because a special incident had occurred—these have the occurrence of a special incident as the ground for their pronouncement. Examples are the suttas Dhammadāyāda, Cūḷasīhanāda, the Candūpama, Puttamaṃsūpama, Dārukkhandhūpama, Aggikkhandhūpama, Phenapiṇḍūpama, and Pāricchattakūpama.

Thus, among these four grounds for delivery, this sutta has the occurrence of a special incident as its ground.

Indeed, this was delivered by the Bhagavān on account of the occurrence of a special incident.

What sort of special incident? The conceit that arose relying on learning.
It is said that five hundred brāhmaṇas, who were masters of the three Vedas, later heard the Blessed One's teaching of the Dhamma. Seeing the danger (ādīnava) in sensual pleasures (kāmesu) and the benefit (ānisaṃsa) in renunciation (nekkhamme), they went forth into homelessness in the presence of the Blessed One. Before long, they mastered the entire word of the Buddha and, relying on their learning (pariyattiṃ nissāya), gave rise to conceit (mānaṃ uppādesuṃ), thinking, “Whatever the Blessed One says (yaṃ yaṃ bhagavā katheti, taṃ taṃ mayaṃ khippameva jānāma), that we quickly understand. The Blessed One does not say anything except by uttering three genders (tīṇi liṅgāni), four cases (cattāri padāni), and seven inflections (satta vibhattiyo). When he speaks in such a way, there is nothing that is a puzzle to us (amhākaṃ gaṇṭhipadaṃ nāma natthi).”

Because they were disrespectful (agāravā hutvā) towards the Blessed One, from then on, they rarely went to attend upon the Blessed One or to listen to the Dhamma.

The Blessed One, knowing the course of their minds (cittacāraṃ ñatvā), and aware that it would be impossible for them to realize the path (maggaṃ) or the fruit (phalaṃ) without uprooting this stake of conceit (mānakhilaṃ anupahacca), made their conceit, born of reliance on their learned scriptures (sutapariyattiṃ nissāya), the occasion for a teaching (aṭṭhuppattiṃ katvā). Skillful in teaching (desanākusalo), the Blessed One, for the purpose of shattering their conceit (mānabhañjanatthaṃ), began the discourse called “The Exposition of the Root of All Dhammas” (sabbadhammamūlapariyāya).

In that context, "sabbadhammamūlapariyāya” means the root exposition (mūlapariyāyaṃ) of all dhammas. "Sabbesaṃ" means without exception (anavasesānaṃ). The word "sabba” expresses the lack of an exception (anavasesavācako). Whichever term it connects with, it indicates the lack of an exception (tassa tassa anavasesataṃ dīpeti), as in, “All form is impermanent (sabbaṃ rūpaṃ aniccaṃ), all feeling is impermanent (sabbā vedanā aniccā)," and "among dhammas included in all aggregates (sabbasakkāyapariyāpannesu dhammesu)."

The word "dhamma" is seen to be used in the senses of scripture (pariyatti), truth (sacca), concentration (samādhi), wisdom (paññā), nature (pakati), intrinsic nature (sabhāva), emptiness (suññatā), merit (puñña), offense (āpatti), the knowable (ñeyya), and so on. In the passage, “Herein, a bhikkhu masters the Dhamma - the suttas, songs," the word "dhamma" occurs in the sense of scripture (pariyatti). In the passage, “Visible in this life, knowable in this life,” it refers to the truths (saccesu). In the passage, “Those Blessed Ones were of such dhammas,” it refers to concentration (samādhimhi).

"Sabbadhammamūlapariyāya." "Sabba." "Dhamma."

"He who has these four dhammas, O monkey king, like you; truth (saccaṃ), dhamma (dhammo), steadfastness (dhiti), and generosity (cāgo—he transcends what is seen."

In the passage, "subject to birth, subject to aging, and also subject to death," it refers to nature (pakatiyaṃ). In the passage "wholesome dhammas," it refers to intrinsic nature (sabhāve). In the passage, “On that occasion there are dhammas," it refers to emptiness (suññatāyaṃ). In the passage, “Dhamma well practiced brings happiness,"

Thus the phrase “the exposition of the root of all dharmas (sabbadhammamūlapariyāya)” signifies the cause designated the particular root-cause of all dharmas (dhammānaṃ), or the teaching of the cause of all dharmas (dhammānaṃ). Thus, the meaning should be observed (daṭṭhabbo).
But this sūtra has to be carefully interpreted. It is not all specific-natured dharmas (sabhāvadhammā) of the four planes that is indicated by the words “all dharmas (sabbadhammāti),” which is to be understood (veditabbā).
But only all dharmas (dhammāva) pertaining to the three planes included in personality (sakkāyapariyāpannā) are to be understood (veditabbā) without remainder (anavasesato); this is the purport (adhippāyoti).

5.
192) The word sādhuka is indeed strengthening, it is also called an order.
Here also, this meaning of strengthening and order should be understood right here.
It occurs in the sense of excellence too.
Indeed, by way of strengthening, listen to this firm Dhamma, taking it up well.
By way of an order, listen to my order.
By way of excellence, it is shown that you should listen to this excellent, auspicious Dhamma in this way.

Attend in your mind, turn towards it, bring it together, that is the meaning; having an undistracted mind, listen, apply it to your mind, that is the intention.
Now, here, “listen to that,” this is a warding off of disturbance of the faculty of sense of hearing.
“Attend carefully in your mind,” this is a warding off of disturbance of the mind-faculty by enjoining strengthening of attention.
The former is the warding off of the grasp of derangement of the phrasing, the latter is the warding off of the grasp of derangement of the meaning.
By the former, he enjoins one to listen to the Dhamma, by the latter, to the retention, examination, etc., of the Dhammas that have been heard.
By the former, he shows: "This Dhamma possesses phrasing; therefore it should be heard."
By the latter, it possesses meaning; therefore it should be attended to carefully in mind.
Or else, having construed the word sādhuka with both words, "Since this Dhamma is deep in doctrine and deep in teaching, therefore listen carefully; since it is deep in meaning and deep in penetration, therefore attend to it carefully in your mind," thus, this construction should be understood.

Attend in your mind!

Listen to that!

“I will speak,” I will explain.
"Listen to that," here, the teaching that has been promised: "I will not only explain concisely, but I will speak of it in detail," is what was said; indeed, these words are expressions of conciseness and detail.
As Vaṅgīsa Thera said:

I will speak!

“He teaches even concisely, he speaks even in detail;

Like the sound of a sāḷikā bird, may insight arise!”
